# Traceability Matrix

**Version:** 1.32
**Date:** 2026-09-02
**Author(s):** John Anderson

This document provides the bi-directional traceability between
the four layers of the `elc` requirements/design/verification
stack:

1. [Software Design Document](SDD.md) (SDD) — architecture and
   module design.
2. [High-Level Requirements](HLRs.md) (HLR) — externally
   observable behaviour the application must provide.
3. [Low-Level Requirements](LLRs.md) (LLR) — per-function
   behavioural contracts that implement the HLRs.
4. [Software Test Plan](STP.md) and the test artefacts
   under [test/](../test/) — Criterion unit tests and Bats
   integration tests that verify each LLR.

It is generated by `tools/render_doc.py` from the
`<traces>` blocks attached to every HLR, LLR, and test in
[doc/Project.xml](Project.xml). The current snapshot covers
**220 HLRs**, **604 LLRs**,
and **1324 tests** across
**60 test files**. Every reference
below is a clickable link to the artifact in its source document.

---

## 6. Coverage Gaps

**There are none.** Both tables below are empty, and that is the
state a release is cut in: every requirement covering behaviour
this release ships has at least one passing test tracing to it
([SDP](SDP.md) §5.5).

A gap appearing here is not a formality. It means a requirement
was written and nothing checks it — and because `make asan`
broadens memory-safety coverage as a side effect of covering a
requirement, a requirement with no test has no memory-safety
coverage either ([STP](STP.md) §7).

### 6.1 LLRs Without a Bound Test

| LLR | Function | HLRs |
| --- | -------- | ---- |

### 6.2 HLRs Without a Bound Test

These HLRs have no test that verifies them directly and no
LLR-bound test in their implementation chain.

| HLR | Name |
| --- | ---- |

### 6.3 Verified by Review Rather Than by Execution

Three claims cannot be settled by any run of the binary. They are
recorded here rather than in the tables above because they are
**not gaps**: each is verified, by a reviewer, and each has a test
asserting as much of it as execution can reach ([STP](STP.md) §2.4,
§7).

| Item | What execution verifies | What review verifies |
| ---- | ----------------------- | -------------------- |
| [`HLR-101`](HLRs.md#HLR-101) | Every finding carries a measurement, a subject, and a published source, and no finding contains imperative text — `no finding proposes a fix` | That the report as a whole offers no advice: an argument about what the words mean, which no assertion can make |
| [`HLR-111`](HLRs.md#HLR-111) | A custom-rule match carries no severity and no attribution — `a rule match carries no severity` | That `elc` holds no opinion of its own about a rule a user supplied |
| [`HLR-121`](HLRs.md#HLR-121) | That the six required query files and their capture names are the contract every shipped module satisfies today | The **cross-release** clause: that the contract does not change under a released tool. A property of the release process, which no run of one release can observe |

A review item is satisfied when the review is recorded. None of
the three counts toward automated coverage, and all three are
listed so the distinction between "verified by review" and
"not verified" is never lost by being left unstated.

---

## 7. Conventions

*   Each HLR carries a `*Trace:*` line in [HLRs.md](HLRs.md)
    listing the SDD sections it implements.
*   Each LLR carries a `*Trace:*` line in [LLRs.md](LLRs.md)
    listing the HLRs it implements (with the HLR's short name).
*   Each test function under [test/](../test/) carries a
    doc-comment block citing the LLR(s) and HLR(s) it verifies,
    mirrored into [Project.xml](Project.xml) as `<traces>`.
*   The [Software Test Plan](STP.md) is the canonical
    catalogue of test purposes; this matrix is the canonical
    cross-reference.
*   This matrix is a snapshot. After modifying any of the four
    upstream documents or the test sources, regenerate it via
    `python3 tools/render_doc.py tools/templates/Traceability.md.j2 Traceability --out doc/Traceability.md`.
